mean_surprisal (0.089 vs 0.088)\\n5. p75_ppl (0.069 vs 0.067)\\n\\n### Code contamination\\n| Depth | Baseline | Temp09 | Δ |\\n|-------|----------|--------|---|\\n| d1 | 5.0% | 7.7% | +2.7pp |\\n| d2 | 9.0% | 11.6% | +2.6pp |\\n| d3 | 12.0% | 13.6% | +1.6pp |\\n\\n### Analysis\\n1. **Depth signal robust across decoding strategies**: temp_only τ=0.9 achieves 76.25% 4-class acc (vs 78.15% baseline), only 1.9pp lower — well within the hypothesis that depth signal is decoding-strategy-agnostic.\\n2. **Ordinal structure preserved**: AUC ordering 0v1 > 1v3 > 1v2 > 2v3 identical to baseline, confirming the difficulty gradient d0≫d1>d2>d3 is robust.\\n3. **d2 remains the weak link**: d2 recall (0.554 in 3-class) is the lowest, consistent with baseline — d2 is the hardest to distinguish from both d1 and d3.\\n4. **Feature importance stable**: Top-5 features identical between strategies with near-identical importance weights, suggesting the same statistical signatures carry depth information regardless of decoding method.\\n5. **Higher code contamination**: Temp09 shows 2-3pp higher code-like content across depths, possibly because temperature-only sampling without top-p truncation preserves more of the distribution tail including code patterns. The filtered 3-class acc (72.52%) mitigates this.\\n6. **Conclusion: SUCCESS** — 4-class acc 76.25% >> 40% threshold, d1v2 AUC 0.8327 >> 0.6 threshold. The 67% progress the worker observed was likely from the depth-1 training phase itself, not an interruption — the training ran to completion despite what the worker's snapshot suggested. The worker's misdiagnosis is critical—they marked the pipeline as killed when it's actually still running on GPU 1 generating depth-2 data, and submitting the restart could cause GPU conflicts. I should also verify whether the olmo_topk50_001 worker correctly held off on submission since that job is still in PREPARING state.

```text
- GPU 1: PID 213402, 9964 MiB, 64% util — there's a discrepancy here. The roadmap shows `olmo_topk50_001` running on cuda:1, but the registry still has it marked as PREPARING, so either the registry wasn't updated when it started or something else entirely is running on that GPU. I need to identify what PID 213402 actually is.

That process on GPU 1 is almost certainly the pipeline that was just launched, but the registry hasn't caught up yet — it's a sync issue between what's actually running and what the registry reflects.

So the main problems are: the registry and roadmap are out of sync (one says RUNNING, one says PREPARING, but the process is definitely there), and I still need to dig into why olmo_temp09_001 failed.

Looking at the timeline more carefully — olmo_temp09_001 started at 20:34, and by 22:54 it was still running Step 4 with depth_2.jsonl growing. But then it got marked as failed with "kill@Step 3 67%", which doesn't match what I was seeing. The kill might have happened when olmo_topk50 was submitted to the same GPU, forcing olmo_temp09 out to free up resources. Looking at the checkpoint directories and logs more carefully, the notes claiming Step 3 was killed at 67% don't match what actually happened — the training completed all 939 steps with a final loss of 1.6294 and the LoRA adapter was saved, so lora_depth_1 should be a complete checkpoint, not an intermediate state. The kill must have occurred during Step 4 (depth-2 generation) instead. The checkpoint timestamps and training logs confirm that lora_depth_1 is fully complete, and depth_2.jsonl was actively growing at 399 lines when the process was terminated, so the actual failure happened mid-Step 4, not at Step 3 as the Agent concluded. This means restarting from Step 3 would redundantly re-finetune depth-1 when the correct recovery point should be Step 4, regenerating depth-2 from the already-finished checkpoint.

The GPU allocation looks fine too—olmo_temp09_001 was on cuda:1 as specified, and olmo_topk50 is also on cuda:1 now, which is within the available resources.

But there's a discrepancy I need to resolve: the ROADMAP shows olmo_topk50_001 as RUNNING, yet the registry still marks it as PREPARING. The process on GPU 1 (PID 213402) is consuming significant memory, and the worker just submitted the job, so this could be a timing issue where the process started but the registry hasn't caught up yet. I need to verify whether the GPU process actually corresponds to olmo_topk50 or if there's something else going on with the worker submission. So the real question is whether olmo_temp09_001 was deliberately killed to free up cuda:1 for olmo_topk50, or if it failed independently and then olmo_topk50 got scheduled on the now-available GPU. I need to cross-reference the timestamps to figure out which happened first — the kill or the submission. Looking at the timeline more carefully, it seems the Agent may have deliberately terminated olmo_temp09_001 to free up GPU memory for the higher-priority olmo_topk50 job, though this wasn't explicitly documented. The key issue I'm noticing is that the crash diagnostics in the notes don't match what actually happened — they mention a specific step failure, but the real problem appears to be resource contention and job prioritization. I need to verify whether lora_depth_1 is actually complete by examining its checkpoint contents directly.
```

_source line 171 · 2026-05-14T23:04:15.605Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have a very clear picture. Here are the critical findings:

**1. Resume script has wrong breakpoint — will delete a complete checkpoint and waste ~3.5 min of compute**

Evidence:
- The log clearly shows Step 3 (depth-1 finetune) completed: "Training complete. Final loss: 1.6294", "LoRA adapter saved to checkpoints/olmo_temp09/lora_depth_1"
- `lora_depth_1/` contains: `adapter_model.safetensors` (6.3MB), `adapter_config.json`, `checkpoint-939` (ALL 939 steps) — this is a COMPLETE checkpoint


- The resume script misidentified where the crash happened. The actual failure occurred during Step 4 (depth-2 generation), not Step 3 — `depth_2.jsonl` has grown to 2.9MB with over 1070 lines, indicating the process was killed mid-generation. The script will unnecessarily delete the completed `lora_depth_1` checkpoint and re-run Step 3 from scratch, wasting compute time.

- Even though re-finetuning takes only ~3.5 minutes, there's a deeper issue: retraining produces a different checkpoint due to GPU nondeterminism, which means the downstream depth-2 generation will diverge from what was originally being generated.
- The real risk is that the partial depth_2.jsonl file (1070 lines) contains data from the original checkpoint, but if the script doesn't delete it before regenerating, I could end up mixing data from two different checkpoints depending on whether generate_next_depth.py overwrites or appends to the file.
- I need to verify the script's behavior to confirm whether this mixing actually happens.

Since I can't run the code directly, I should flag this as a potential issue. But there's something more concerning: depth_2.jsonl is currently 2.9MB and still growing, even though the olmo_temp09_001 process was marked as failed at 22:58:51. The file's modification timestamp shows it was updated 5 minutes after the failure, growing from 602KB to 2.9MB. Either the process wasn't actually killed and is still running as a zombie, or something else is writing to this file.

Looking at the GPU state now, GPU 1 has PID 213402 using 9964 MiB, and the olmo_topk50_001 worker was just submitted. The growing depth_2.jsonl file and its recent timestamp suggest the old olmo_temp09 process might still be alive and generating data. If the Agent marked it as failed but didn't actually terminate it, we could have a zombie process consuming GPU memory alongside the new worker.

With only two processes visible on the GPUs, PID 213402 on GPU 1 could be the surviving olmo_temp09 process rather than the new olmo_topk50 job. This would mean the Agent failed to properly kill the old process, and when olmo_topk50 tries to start on the same GPU, we'd hit a memory conflict. I need to verify which process is actually running.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Interesting development. The Agent has corrected course:

1. **olmo_temp09_001 is now back to RUNNING** (not failed anymore). The notes say: "False failure 修正: cron 误报进程退出，实际 pipeline 仍在 cuda:1 活跃。当前进度: Step 4 (generate depth-2, PID 213402)。之前 Step 3 finetune depth-1 已完成。retry_count=1 因为经历了 DONE→PREPARING→RUNNING 状态回退。"
   - The cron system had a false alarm — the process never actually exited, it's still actively running on cuda:1 at Step 4. Since olmo_temp09_001 is still going, olmo_temp09_002 needs to be cancelled.

2. **Current GPU allocation**: GPU 0 is handling gpt2xl_topk50_001 at 66% utilization with 15629 MiB, while GPU 1 has olmo_temp09_001 back online consuming 9973 MiB.

3. **The scheduling conflict**: olmo_topk50_001 is stuck in PREPARING state despite the worker completing its submission, and there's no corresponding process on the GPUs. The roadmap expected it to run on cuda:1, but that's already occupied by olmo_temp09_001 — they can't coexist on the same GPU.

4. **Tracing the sequence**: The cron job falsely reported olmo_temp09_001 as dead, triggering a rerun (olmo_temp09_002) and scheduling olmo_topk50 for cuda:1. The worker submitted successfully, but then the agent discovered olmo_temp09_001 was still actually running and corrected it back to RUNNING status. This likely left olmo_topk50 in limbo — submitted but unable to launch because its intended GPU slot was never freed.

5. **Checking the actual state**: The GPU processes show only 2 running jobs (one per GPU), so olmo_topk50 isn't executing anywhere. If it had been submitted to cuda:1 while olmo_temp09 was still there, there should be 3 processes visible, but there aren't.

6. **Most likely scenario**: olmo_topk50 was submitted successfully (the worker completed with status=done), but the job either crashed immediately due to GPU contention, or it's stuck in a pre-GPU phase like downloading/loading weights. The core issue is that olmo_temp09_001 never actually stopped running, so the GPU slot olmo_topk50 needed was never available. I'm seeing there's cleanup work needed—olmo_temp09_002 should be cancelled since its parent process is still active, and olmo_topk50_001's actual submission status is unclear. The bigger issue is that olmo_temp09_001 still has the old failure reason in its metadata even though the Agent correctly identified it as a false alarm and restored it to running.

There's also a discrepancy in the previous failure notes about lora_depth_1—the checkpoint was marked as incomplete (中间态) but the server shows it actually finished with checkpoint-939 and all the necessary adapter files present, so that characterization was inaccurate from the start.

The ROADMAP is also out of sync with the registry: it lists olmo_topk50_001 as RUNNING when it's actually still PREPARING, and now that olmo_temp09_001 has recovered to RUNNING status, the GPU scheduling information is stale.

I've confirmed the pipeline is still active by checking the data growth—the depth_2.jsonl file went from 303 to 399 to 1070 lines, so it's definitely processing. Now I need to write up my findings for the Director, highlighting that while the agent correctly identified and fixed the false failure for olmo_temp09_001, there are several stale status indicators and documentation inconsistencies that need addressing.

The main issues are the conflicting registry entries, the outdated ROADMAP GPU schedule, and the incorrect failure diagnosis from earlier (the step count and lora_depth_1 status were both wrong). This is a warning-level report since there's no data corruption or GPU violations—the experiment itself is running fine. Let me quickly check on olmo_topk50_001's status before finalizing.
```

```text
Now I have the full picture. Let me summarize:

1. **olmo_temp09_001**: Agent correctly identified false failure, restored to RUNNING. PID 213402 on GPU 1, currently at Step 4 (generating depth-2, 1070/5000 lines).

2. **olmo_temp09_002**: Agent already noted it's cancelled ("取消: olmo_temp09_001 实际仍在运行（false failure），无需重跑。"). Good.

3. **olmo_topk50_001**: Still PREPARING, no log file exists, data directory only has symlinked depth_0.jsonl. 

The submission worker for olmo_topk50_001 reported completion, but no actual training process started—the GPU state shows only the two already-running jobs, and the Agent likely cancelled this submission to prevent conflicts with the still-executing olmo_temp09_001.

Now I'm reconsidering the timeline: the worker completed before the Agent even discovered the false failure, so the question becomes whether olmo_topk50 was killed after starting or never launched at all. The absence of any log file and no GPU process suggests it either failed to start or was preemptively cancelled.

Either way, olmo_topk50_001 needs to be rescheduled once the current jobs finish. Right now cuda:0 is running gpt2xl_topk50 and cuda:1 has olmo_temp09_001 resuming, so after both complete, cuda:0 should pick up dcol_deberta_003 and cuda:1 should get olmo_topk50_001. The ROADMAP needs updating to reflect this corrected state.

I should also verify whether the previous failure diagnosis was accurate—the Agent's notes mention the pipeline was killed at Step 3 during finetuning at 67% completion.

Looking at the actual server state though, the checkpoint is at 939 (not 626), the adapter model exists, the logs show training completed with a final loss of 1.6294, and depth_2.jsonl is actively growing with over 1000 lines. So the original failure report was completely wrong—the cron job misreported the status, and the Agent was working from stale or incorrect log data. The corrective note flagged this as a false failure, which is right, but the old failure_reason field still contains the inaccurate information.

Now I'm ready to write up my review with the key findings: the Agent correctly caught and fixed the false failure on olmo_temp09_001, but olmo_topk50_001 never actually started executing despite being marked as submitted. I'm checking the actual GPU memory allocation and confirming that both processes are properly constrained to their assigned devices without overflow or contention issues.
```

There's also a GPU assignment discrepancy: the cmdline shows `--gpu 0`, but the spec specifies `gpu: cuda:1`, and nvidia-smi confirms PID 213402 is running on GPU 1. This suggests the script defaults to GPU 0, but the actual assignment likely happens through CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES being set to 1 by the job submission, making device 0 appear as the visible GPU within that constrained space.

```text
Now I need to summarize my findings: the agent correctly fixed the false failure, but there are three issues to flag—the registry still has stale failure metadata from the earlier step, the resume script poses a deletion risk, and olmo_temp09_002 should be cancelled since olmo_temp09_001 is running again.
```
